# DTC C0063-F0 (Si) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC C0063-F0 : Yaw Rate Sensor Malfunction

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

C0063-F0 Yaw Rate Sensor Malfunction

DTC (Adaptive Damper System)

- SRS unit check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Substitute a known-good SRS unit . -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Clear the DTC with the HDS. Clear DTCs -5. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -6. Test-drive the vehicle under these conditions: Vehicle speed at 31 mph (50 km/h) or more for 10 seconds or more. Change lanes a few times. NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift. -7. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame C0063-F0 Yaw Rate Sensor Malfunction Is DTC C0063-F0 indicated? YES Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the adaptive damper control unit 24P connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting. If they are OK, replace the adaptive damper control unit . NO Replace the original SRS unit .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Substitute a known-good SRS unit .

-3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-4. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

Clear DTCs

-5. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-6. Test-drive the vehicle under these conditions:

- Vehicle speed at 31 mph (50 km/h) or more for 10 seconds or more.

- Change lanes a few times.

NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift.

-7. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

C0063-F0 Yaw Rate Sensor Malfunction

Is DTC C0063-F0 indicated?

YES

Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the adaptive damper control unit 24P connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting. If they are OK, replace the adaptive damper control unit .

NO

Replace the original SRS unit .
````

Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector Left-front suspension stroke sensor 3P connector -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle ON mode Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector: disconnected Left-front suspension stroke sensor 3P connector: disconnected Test point 1 Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector No. 24 Test point 2 Body ground Is there about 5 V or more? YES Repair a short to power in the wire between the left-front suspension stroke sensor and the adaptive damper control unit. NO The ADJ SNSR FR SIG wire is OK. Go to step 3.

Is there continuity? YES The ADS SG wire is OK. Go to step 4. NO Repair an open in the wire between the left-front suspension stroke sensor and the adaptive damper control unit.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

# DTC C1650-62, C1652-62, C1653-62 (Type-R) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC C1650-62 : Left-Front Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality

DTC C1652-62 : Left-Rear Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality

DTC C1653-62 : Right-Rear Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

C1650-62 Left-Front Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality

C1652-62 Left-Rear Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality

C1653-62 Right-Rear Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality

DTC (Adaptive Damper System)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. Clear DTCs -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode. -4. Test-drive the vehicle at 16 mph (25 km/h) or more for at least 60 seconds. NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame C1650-62 Left-Front Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality C1652-62 Left-Rear Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality C1653-62 Right-Rear Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality Is DTC C1650-62, C1652-62, or C1653-62 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the freeze data/on-board snapshot.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

Clear DTCs

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode.

-4. Test-drive the vehicle at 16 mph (25 km/h) or more for at least 60 seconds.

NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift.

-5. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

C1650-62 Left-Front Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality

C1652-62 Left-Rear Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality

C1653-62 Right-Rear Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality

Is DTC C1650-62, C1652-62, or C1653-62 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the freeze data/on-board snapshot.

- Acceleration sensor check -1. Substitute a known-good acceleration sensor . -2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -3. Clear the DTC with the HDS. Clear DTCs -4. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode. -5. Test-drive the vehicle at 16 mph (25 km/h) or more for at least 60 seconds. NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift. -6. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame C1650-62 Left-Front Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality C1652-62 Left-Rear Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality C1653-62 Right-Rear Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality Is DTC C1650-62, C1652-62, or C1653-62 indicated? YES Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the adaptive damper control unit 24P connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting. If they are OK, replace the adaptive damper control unit . NO Replace the appropriate original acceleration sensor .

-1. Substitute a known-good acceleration sensor .

-2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-3. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

Clear DTCs

-4. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode.

-5. Test-drive the vehicle at 16 mph (25 km/h) or more for at least 60 seconds.

NOTE: Drive the vehicle on the road, not on a lift.

-6. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

C1650-62 Left-Front Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality

C1652-62 Left-Rear Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality

C1653-62 Right-Rear Acceleration Sensor Difference Abnormality

Is DTC C1650-62, C1652-62, or C1653-62 indicated?

YES

Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the adaptive damper control unit 24P connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting. If they are OK, replace the adaptive damper control unit .

NO

Replace the appropriate original acceleration sensor .
````

````text
# DTC U0029-00 (Si (Adaptive Damper Control Unit)) (2017 2018 2019)

DTC U0029-00 : F-CAN Bus-Off Malfunction

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Troubleshoot any PGM-FI system DTCs first .

- This DTC is stored by communication failure between the F-CAN line and the adaptive damper control unit. When DTC U0029-00 is indicated, the F-CAN line has already returned to normal. Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the connectors. If the connectors are OK, clear the DTC and following this troubleshooting.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

U0029-00 F-CAN Bus-Off Malfunction

DTC (Adaptive Damper System)

- Connector check -1. Check for loose terminal and poor connection at the following connector. Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector Is the connector OK? YES Go to step 2. NO Repair loose terminal or poor connection. Then clear the DTC by using the HDS and recheck DTC U0029-00. If DTC U0029-00 is not confirmed, troubleshooting is complete.

-1. Check for loose terminal and poor connection at the following connector.

Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector

Is the connector OK?

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Repair loose terminal or poor connection. Then clear the DTC by using the HDS and recheck DTC U0029-00. If DTC U0029-00 is not confirmed, troubleshooting is complete.

- Adaptive damper control unit communication check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, connect the HDS (Honda Diagnostic System) to the data link connector (DLC) . -2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -3. Check for communication between the adaptive damper control unit and the HDS. Is there communication between the adaptive damper control unit and the HDS? YES Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the freeze data/on-board snapshot. NO Go to DLC circuit troubleshooting .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, connect the HDS (Honda Diagnostic System) to the data link connector (DLC) .

-2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-3. Check for communication between the adaptive damper control unit and the HDS.

Is there communication between the adaptive damper control unit and the HDS?

YES

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the freeze data/on-board snapshot.

NO

Go to DLC circuit troubleshooting .
````

# DTC U0029-00 (Si (Adaptive Damper Control Unit)) (2020 2021)

DTC U0029-00 : F-CAN Bus-Off Malfunction

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Troubleshoot any PGM-FI system DTCs first .

- This DTC is stored by communication failure between the F-CAN line and the adaptive damper control unit. When DTC U0029-00 is indicated, the F-CAN line has already returned to normal. Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the connectors. If the connectors are OK, clear the DTC and following this troubleshooting.

Are any DTCs indicated?

YES

Go to the troubleshooting for CAN gateway system DTC(s) .

NO

Go to step 2.

- F-CAN circuit communication check (Receiving control unit) -1. Select the FUNCTION TEST in the CAN gateway with the HDS, then select the F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check. F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check Does the CAN gateway detect the adaptive damper control unit Bus channels B? Adaptive damper control unit (Bus B) is Not Available Go to step 3. Adaptive damper control unit is Available for Bus B Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the adaptive damper control unit connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting. If they are OK, replace the adaptive damper control unit .

# DTC U0029-00 (Type-R, With ACC (Adaptive Damper Control Unit)) (2020 2021)

DTC U0029-00 : F-CAN Bus-Off Malfunction

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Troubleshoot any PGM-FI system DTCs first .

- This DTC is stored by communication failure between the F-CAN line and the adaptive damper control unit. When DTC U0029-00 is indicated, the F-CAN line has already returned to normal. Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the connectors. If the connectors are OK, clear the DTC and following this troubleshooting.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

U0029-00 F-CAN Bus-Off Malfunction

DTC (Adaptive Damper System)

- CAN gateway system DTC check -1. Check for CAN gateway system DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Are any DTCs indicated? YES Go to the troubleshooting for CAN gateway system DTC(s) . NO Go to step 2.

YES

Go to the troubleshooting for CAN gateway system DTC(s) .

NO

Go to step 2.

- F-CAN circuit communication check (Receiving control unit) -1. Select the FUNCTION TEST in the CAN gateway with the HDS, then select the F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check. F-CAN Bus Connected Unit Check Does the CAN gateway detect the adaptive damper control unit Bus channels B? Adaptive damper control unit (Bus B) is Not Available Go to step 3. Adaptive damper control unit is Available for Bus B Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the adaptive damper control unit connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting. If they are OK, replace the adaptive damper control unit .

# DTC U0029-00 (Type-R, Without ACC (Adaptive Damper Control Unit)) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC U0029-00 : F-CAN Bus-Off Malfunction

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Troubleshoot any PGM-FI system DTCs first .

- This DTC is stored by communication failure between the F-CAN line and the adaptive damper control unit. When DTC U0029-00 is indicated, the F-CAN line has already returned to normal. Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the connectors. If the connectors are OK, clear the DTC and following this troubleshooting.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

U0029-00 F-CAN Bus-Off Malfunction

DTC (Adaptive Damper System)

- Connector check -1. Check for loose terminal and poor connection at the following connector. Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector Is the connector OK? YES Go to step 2. NO Repair loose terminal or poor connection. Then clear the DTC by using the HDS and recheck DTC U0029-00. If DTC U0029-00 is not confirmed, troubleshooting is complete.

-1. Check for loose terminal and poor connection at the following connector.

Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector

Is the connector OK?

YES

Go to step 2.

NO

Repair loose terminal or poor connection. Then clear the DTC by using the HDS and recheck DTC U0029-00. If DTC U0029-00 is not confirmed, troubleshooting is complete.

- Adaptive damper control unit communication check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, connect the HDS (Honda Diagnostic System) to the data link connector (DLC) . -2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -3. Check for communication between the adaptive damper control unit and the HDS. Is there communication between the adaptive damper control unit and the HDS? YES Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the freeze data/on-board snapshot. NO Go to DLC circuit troubleshooting .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, connect the HDS (Honda Diagnostic System) to the data link connector (DLC) .

-2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-3. Check for communication between the adaptive damper control unit and the HDS.

Is there communication between the adaptive damper control unit and the HDS?

YES

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the freeze data/on-board snapshot.

NO

Go to DLC circuit troubleshooting .
````

# DTC U0100-00, U0122-00, U0131-00, U0151-00 (Si (Adaptive Damper Control Unit)) (2017 2018 2019)

DTC U0100-00 : CAN Communication Failure with PCM/TCM

DTC U0122-00 : F-CAN Communication With VSA Modulator-Control Unit Malfunction

DTC U0131-00 : F-CAN Communication With EPS Malfunction

DTC U0151-00 : CAN Communication Failure with SRS

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Refer to the DTC code shown on the display, and then inspect the connectors and terminals based on the instructions in the table below.

- These DTC(s) may be stored due to the power/ground circuit failure of the appropriate transmitting control unit. Before this troubleshooting, check the power/ground circuit of the appropriate transmitting control unit first.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

U0100-00 CAN Communication Failure with PCM/TCM

U0122-00 F-CAN Communication With VSA Modulator-Control Unit Malfunction

U0131-00 F-CAN Communication With EPS Malfunction

U0151-00 CAN Communication Failure with SRS

DTC (Adaptive Damper System)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. Clear DTCs -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode. -4. Wait for 60 seconds or more. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame U0100-00 CAN Communication Failure with PCM/TCM U0122-00 F-CAN Communication With VSA Modulator-Control Unit Malfunction U0131-00 F-CAN Communication With EPS Malfunction U0151-00 CAN Communication Failure with SRS Is DTC U0100-00, U0122-00, U0131-00, or U0151-00 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the transmitting control units, and adaptive damper control unit. If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the freeze data/on-board snapshot.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

Clear DTCs

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode.

-4. Wait for 60 seconds or more.

-5. Check for DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

U0100-00 CAN Communication Failure with PCM/TCM

U0122-00 F-CAN Communication With VSA Modulator-Control Unit Malfunction

U0131-00 F-CAN Communication With EPS Malfunction

U0151-00 CAN Communication Failure with SRS

Is DTC U0100-00, U0122-00, U0131-00, or U0151-00 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the transmitting control units, and adaptive damper control unit. If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the freeze data/on-board snapshot.

- Open wire check (F-CAN_H line, F-CAN_L line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector -3. Disconnect the appropriate transmitting control unit connector. NOTE: Before disconnecting the SRS unit connector, do the 12 volt battery terminal disconnection procedure and wait for at least 3 minutes. Before disconnecting the PCM connector, jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. DTC Transmitting Control Unit U0100-00 PCM connector A (50P) U0122-00 VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector U0131-00 EPS control unit connector B (6P) U0151-00 SRS unit connector A (39P) -4. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector: disconnected Transmitting control unit connector(s) for indicated DTCs: disconnected DTC Circuit Name Test point 1 (Receiving control unit) Test point 2 (Transmitting control unit) Connector Terminal Connector Terminal U0100-00 F-CAN_H Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector No. 19 PCM connector A (50P) No. 37 F-CAN_L No. 20 No. 36 U0122-00 F-CAN_H Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector No. 19 VSA modulator-control unit 46P connector No. 20 F-CAN_L No. 20 No. 21 U0131-00 F-CAN_H Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector No. 19 EPS control unit connector B (6P) No. 3 F-CAN_L No. 20 No.

DTC Operation For Transmitting Control Unit U0100-00 Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the adaptive damper control unit and the PCM. Substitute a known-good PCM , then recheck. U0122-00 Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the adaptive damper control unit and the VSA modulator-control unit. Replace the VSA modulator-control unit . U0151-00 Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the adaptive damper control unit and the SRS unit. Substitute a known-good SRS unit , then recheck. According to the detected DTC on the following table, substitute or replace the correspond control unit.

DTC | Operation For Transmitting Control Unit

U0100-00 | Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the CAN gateway and the PCM. Substitute a known-good PCM , then recheck. If DTC U0100-00 goes away and the PCM was substituted, replace the original PCM .

NO

Repair an open in the F-CAN A_H wire and/or the F-CAN A_L wire between the transmitting control unit and the CAN gateway.

# DTC U0100-00, U0122-00, U0131-00, U0151-00 (Type-R, With ACC (Adaptive Damper Control Unit)) (2020 2021)

DTC U0100-00 : F-CAN Communication With PCM/TCM Malfunction

DTC U0122-00 : F-CAN Communication With VSA Modulator-Control Unit Malfunction

DTC U0131-00 : F-CAN Communication With EPS Malfunction

DTC U0151-00 : F-CAN Communication With SRS Malfunction

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Refer to the DTC code shown on the display, and then inspect the connectors and terminals based on the instructions in the table below.

- These DTC(s) may be stored due to the power/ground circuit failure of the appropriate transmitting control unit. Before this troubleshooting, check the power/ground circuit of the appropriate transmitting control unit first.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

U0100-00 F-CAN Communication With PCM/TCM Malfunction

U0122-00 F-CAN Communication With VSA Modulator-Control Unit Malfunction

U0131-00 F-CAN Communication With EPS Malfunction

U0151-00 F-CAN Communication With SRS Malfunction

DTC (Adaptive Damper System)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. Clear DTCs -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode. -4. Wait for 60 seconds or more. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame U0100-00 F-CAN Communication With PCM/TCM Malfunction U0122-00 F-CAN Communication With VSA Modulator-Control Unit Malfunction U0131-00 F-CAN Communication With EPS Malfunction U0151-00 F-CAN Communication With SRS Malfunction Is DTC U0100-00, U0122-00, U0131-00, or U0151-00 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the transmitting control units, and adaptive damper control unit. If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the freeze data/on-board snapshot.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

# DTC U0100-00, U0122-00, U0131-00, U0151-00 (Type-R, Without ACC (Adaptive Damper Control Unit)) (2017 2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC U0100-00 : CAN Communication Failure with PCM/TCM

DTC U0122-00 : F-CAN Communication With VSA Modulator-Control Unit Malfunction

DTC U0131-00 : F-CAN Communication With EPS Malfunction

DTC U0151-00 : CAN Communication Failure with SRS

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Refer to the DTC code shown on the display, and then inspect the connectors and terminals based on the instructions in the table below.

- These DTC(s) may be stored due to the power/ground circuit failure of the appropriate transmitting control unit. Before this troubleshooting, check the power/ground circuit of the appropriate transmitting control unit first.

DTC Description | DTC | Freeze Frame

U0100-00 CAN Communication Failure with PCM/TCM

U0122-00 F-CAN Communication With VSA Modulator-Control Unit Malfunction

U0131-00 F-CAN Communication With EPS Malfunction

U0151-00 CAN Communication Failure with SRS

DTC (Adaptive Damper System)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. Clear DTCs -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode. -4. Wait for 60 seconds or more. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame U0100-00 CAN Communication Failure with PCM/TCM U0122-00 F-CAN Communication With VSA Modulator-Control Unit Malfunction U0131-00 F-CAN Communication With EPS Malfunction U0151-00 CAN Communication Failure with SRS Is DTC U0100-00, U0122-00, U0131-00, or U0151-00 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the transmitting control units, and adaptive damper control unit. If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the freeze data/on-board snapshot.

U0151-00 | F-CAN_H | Adaptive damper control unit 24P connector | No. 19 | SRS unit connector A (39P) | No. 34

F-CAN_L | No. 20 | No. 35

Is there continuity?

YES

The F-CAN_H wire and the F-CAN_L wire are OK. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting.

According to the detected DTC on the following table, substitute or replace the correspond control unit.

DTC | Operation For Transmitting Control Unit

U0100-00 | Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the adaptive damper control unit and the PCM. Substitute a known-good PCM , then recheck. If DTC U0100-00 goes away and the PCM was substituted, replace the original PCM .

U0122-00 | Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the adaptive damper control unit and the VSA modulator-control unit. Replace the VSA modulator-control unit .

U0131-00 | Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the adaptive damper control unit and the EPS motor/control unit. Substitute a known-good EPS motor/control unit , then recheck. If DTC U0131-00 goes away and the EPS motor/control unit was substituted, replace the original EPS motor/control unit .

U0151-00 | Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the adaptive damper control unit and the SRS unit. Substitute a known-good SRS unit , then recheck. If DTC U0151-00 goes away and the SRS unit was substituted, replace the original SRS unit .

NO

Repair an open in the F-CAN_H wire and/or the F-CAN_L wire between the adaptive damper control unit and the appropriate transmitting control unit.
````

U3003-17 Adaptive Damper Control Unit +B Voltage High Failure

U3006-17 Adaptive Damper Control Unit IG (+B) Voltage High Failure

Is DTC U3003-17 and/or U3006-17 indicated?

YES

Check for loose terminals and poor connections in the adaptive damper control unit 24P connector. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting. If they are OK, replace the adaptive damper control unit .

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Refer to intermittent failures troubleshooting . If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the freeze data/on-board snapshot.
````

DTC (Adaptive Damper System)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. Clear DTCs -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, then to the ON mode. -4. Wait for 30 seconds or more. -5. Check for DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description DTC Freeze Frame U3006-16 Adaptive Damper Control Unit IG (+B) Voltage Low Failure Is DTC U3006-16 indicated? If the freeze data/on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the freeze data/on-board snapshot.

NO

NO

# TPMS Symptom Troubleshooting Index (TPMS)

Symptom | Diagnostic procedure | Also check for

HDS does not communicate with the VSA modulator-control unit or the vehicle | Troubleshoot the DLC circuit .

Without TPMS switch: Deflation warning system cannot be Initialized | Check for VSA system DTCs . Check for the VSA indicator. If VSA indicator turns on, do the VSA system symptom troubleshooting "ABS indicator, brake system indicator, and VSA indicator do not go off" . Do the gauge control module self-diagnostic function . If the VSA modulator-control unit the symptom is still present, replace the VSA modulator-control unit .

- Check for VSA system DTCs .

- Check for the VSA indicator. If VSA indicator turns on, do the VSA system symptom troubleshooting "ABS indicator, brake system indicator, and VSA indicator do not go off" .

- Do the gauge control module self-diagnostic function .

- If the VSA modulator-control unit the symptom is still present, replace the VSA modulator-control unit .

With TPMS switch: TPMS cannot be calibrated | Symptom troubleshooting . If the symptom is still present, replace the VSA modulator-control unit .

- Symptom troubleshooting .

- If the symptom is still present, replace the VSA modulator-control unit .

TPMS warning is indicated | Check for VSA system DTCs . Check for the VSA indicator. If VSA indicator turns on, do the VSA system symptom troubleshooting "ABS indicator, brake system indicator (red), brake system indicator (amber), and VSA indicator do not go off" . If the VSA modulator-control unit the symptom is still present, replace the VSA modulator-control unit .

- Check for VSA system DTCs .

- Check for the VSA indicator. If VSA indicator turns on, do the VSA system symptom troubleshooting "ABS indicator, brake system indicator (red), brake system indicator (amber), and VSA indicator do not go off" .

- If the VSA modulator-control unit the symptom is still present, replace the VSA modulator-control unit .

Low tire pressure/TPMS indicator comes on | Check the tire size and tire pressure according to tire information. Do the TPMS calibration .

- Check the tire size and tire pressure according to tire information.

- Do the TPMS calibration .

Low tire pressure/TPMS indicator does not come on when the tire pressure has decreased | See general troubleshooting information "condition for data rejection, and case TPMS cannot operate normally" (Even if a system is normal, there is a possibility that cannot detect low tire pressure) . Do the gauge control module self-diagnostic function . Do the TPMS calibration . If the VSA modulator-control unit the symptom is still present, replace the VSA modulator-control unit .

- See general troubleshooting information "condition for data rejection, and case TPMS cannot operate normally" (Even if a system is normal, there is a possibility that cannot detect low tire pressure) .

- Do the gauge control module self-diagnostic function .

- Do the TPMS calibration .

## Chunk 9673: TPMS Calibration: Procedure

- Title: TPMS Calibration: Procedure
- Source path: `pages\12281.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_1ab6b883da63`
- Images: `images\GHH412634.jpeg`, `images\GHH412635.jpeg`, `images\GHH412636.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\13625.html`

### Full Text

````text
# TPMS Calibration: Procedure

NOTE:

- Make sure the tire pressure is properly adjusted to the specified tire pressure listed on the doorjamb label before doing the TPMS calibration.

- The calibration begins when the TPMS switch is pressed. The calibration is completed after driving in an ideal driving condition 28 to 65 mph (45 to 105 km/h) *1 or 22 to 65 mph (35 to 105) *2, driving steadily without much acceleration or deceleration) for about 20 minutes. Full functionality of the system cannot be performed properly if the calibration is not completed. *1: 2/4-door *2: 5-door

*1: 2/4-door

*2: 5-door

- Do the TPMS calibration after doing the following items.

- Adjust the tire pressure. Rotating the tires. Replacing the tire(s). Update the VSA modulator-control unit. Replace the VSA modulator-control unit.

- Adjust the tire pressure.

- Rotating the tires.

- Replacing the tire(s).

- Update the VSA modulator-control unit.

- Replace the VSA modulator-control unit.

- Calibration check method. Check if the calibration is completed by doing the following items.

Check if the calibration is completed by doing the following items.

- Stop the vehicle and shift into neutral (M/T) or P or N (CVT). Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode and wait to see the low pressure indicator come on for 2 seconds and then off. Do not drive the vehicle and wait for 45 seconds as is. If the calibration is completed, the indicator does not come on. If the calibration is not yet completed, then the indicator comes on for 2 seconds and then goes off.

- Stop the vehicle and shift into neutral (M/T) or P or N (CVT).

- Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

- Turn the vehicle to the ON mode and wait to see the low pressure indicator come on for 2 seconds and then off.

- Do not drive the vehicle and wait for 45 seconds as is.

- If the calibration is completed, the indicator does not come on. If the calibration is not yet completed, then the indicator comes on for 2 seconds and then goes off.

The calibration check process gets interrupted if the vehicle moves. Do the troubleshooting if the indicator does not go off after 2 seconds, keeps blinking or stays on when the vehicle is in the ON mode.

- TPMS - Calibrate (Without TPMS Switch) Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. With display audio type (7-inch screen) 1. Make sure the tires are inflated to the specified tire pressure listed on the doorjamb label. 2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. NOTE: Vehicle must be stopped with the transmission in neutral (M/T) or P or N (CVT). 3. Select the HOME button on the screen (A). 4. Select the SETTINGS. 5. Select the VEHICLE. 6. ## Chunk 9677: TPMS System Description - Control

- Title: TPMS System Description - Control
- Source path: `pages\12283.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_539a868ad99d`
- Images: `images\GHH412640.jpeg`, `images\GHH412641.jpeg`, `images\GHH412642.jpeg`, `images\GHH412643.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\13627.html`

### Full Text

````text
# TPMS System Description - Control

Overview

The Indirect TPMS establishes a baseline tire pressure reading using the wheel speed sensors and indirectly monitors tire pressure to detect pressure loss in any of the four tires. The system does not monitor the tires when driving at low speed.

The system uses these two methods to determine loss of tire pressure:

- Wheel speed differential

- Peak tire resonance

Wheel Speed Differential

When monitoring tire pressure using the wheel speed differential method, the TPMS module compares the speed of the wheels in three different configurations:

- Diagonal comparison between the front and rear wheels

- Comparison between the two front and two rear wheels

- Comparison between the two right and two left wheels

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Tire diameter shrinks as the tire deflates, causing the wheel speed to increase.

Consequently, if the difference in wheel speeds exceeds the threshold, the low tire pressure/TPMS indicator turns on as shown in the step-to-step diagram below.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Peak Tire Resonance

Peak resonance frequency value learned during calibration becomes the initial value. The peak resonance frequency method compares the initial peak resonance frequency value of the two front tires and the present peak resonance frequency value of the two front tires, and detects reduced pressure of the tires. If the present peak resonance value drops below the threshold, the low tire pressure/TPMS indicator turns ON as shown in the step-to-step diagram below.

Resonance frequency is corrected based on the outside air temperature sensor information, because resonance frequency is changed by the outside air temperature.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The TPMS module compares the peak resonance values learned at calibration. If the value drops below the threshold, the low tire pressure/TPMS indicator turns on.

Temporary Suspension

Certain situations, such as when the vehicle is turning, accelerating, decelerating, or braking will temporarily prevent the system from monitoring tire pressures due to varying tire loads.

Calibration

A calibration process is required for the TPMS module to learn the initial rolling radii of the four tires and peak tire resonance values. The calibration procedure must be performed any time the tire pressures are adjusted, or when the tires are rotated or replaced. The TPMS calibration procedure is initiated by pressing the TPMS switch (if equipped) or doing customization (without TPMS switch).

Upon the completion of system calibration, vehicle mileage and calibration data is stored in the VSA modulator-control unit. If larger diameter accessory wheels have been installed, the TPMS system needs to be calibrated to recognize the new wheel size. This procedure is performed using the HDS threshold rewriting function.
````

## Chunk 9678: TPMS System Description - Overview

- Title: TPMS System Description - Overview
- Source path: `pages\12284.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_6a3c9a917045`
- Images: none
- Duplicate sources: `pages\13628.html`

### Full Text

````text
# TPMS System Description - Overview

The Indirect Tire Pressure Monitoring System (TPMS) establishes a baseline tire pressure reading using the wheel speed sensors and indirectly monitors tire pressure to detect pressure loss in any of the four tires. The low tire pressure/TPMS indicator turns ON appear when the tire pressure of any tire, or tires, falls below the specified threshold or a system malfunction is detected.

The system is controlled by the VSA modulator-control unit.

The TPMS consists of the following primary components:

- VSA modulator-control unit

- Wheel speed sensors

- Outside air temperature sensor

- System indicator

For locations of each component on vehicle, refer to Component Location Index , and for functions of indicator, refer to How to Troubleshoot the TPMS .
````

## Chunk 9679: TPMS System Description - System Diagram

- Title: TPMS System Description - System Diagram
- Source path: `pages\12285.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_b54cdafced5d`
- Images: `images\GHH412644.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\13629.html`

### Full Text

````text
# TPMS System Description - System Diagram

The TPMS module is integrated into the VSA modulator-control unit. In order to control tire pressure monitoring, the TPMS module first receives inputs from the VSA system, which includes the wheel speed sensors, the hydraulic pressure sensor, the PCM, the SRS unit, the EPS control unit, and the gauge control module. Then, the TPMS module sends commands to the gauge control module through the VSA modulator-control unit and issues warnings to the driver.

For locations of each component on vehicle, refer to Component Location Index:

- VSA modulator-control unit, Wheel speed sensors, TPMS switch, Outside air temperature sensor

- EPS control unit

- PCM

- SRS unit

- Gauge control module

- Climate control unit

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.
````

## Chunk 9687: DTC P0501 (K20C2 (CVT System))

- Title: DTC P0501 (K20C2 (CVT System))
- Source path: `pages\12293.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_567ad3a098ae`
- Images: `images\GHH399772.jpeg`, `images\GHH399773.jpeg`, `images\GHH399774.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\19422.html`

### Full Text

````text
# DTC P0501 (K20C2 (CVT System))

DTC P0501: CVT Speed Sensor Circuit Range/Performance

General Description

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The CVT speed sensor is attached to the outside of the transmission housing. The CVT speed sensor detects the number of revolutions of the secondary driven gear and sends a pulsing signal to the transmission control module (TCM). The TCM converts the pulsing signal into vehicle speed. If no pulses occur with the secondary driven gear rotating, the TCM detects a malfunction that may be caused by an open, an intermittent open, or a short to ground. Based on the correlation between the CVT speed sensor and the CVT drive pulley speed sensor, the TCM detects a malfunction and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Continuous

Sequence | None

Duration | 4 seconds or more

DTC Type | One drive cycle, MIL on, Shift position indicator blinks

Enable Conditions

Condition | Minimum | Maximum

Engine speed [Engine Speed] | 500 rpm | -

Drive pulley speed [Input Shaft (Drive Pulley) Speed rpm] | 200 rpm | -

12 volt battery voltage [Battery Voltage] | 10.0 V | -

Shift position/mode | Without paddle shifter | D, S, L

With paddle shifter | D, S

Other | Rotation speed [Secondary Shaft Speed 1 (rpm)] measured by CVT speed sensor is 200 rpm or more

No electrical noise in the CVT speed sensor output circuit

Decelerate and fuel cut-off for at least 3 seconds

[ ]:HDS Parameter

Malfunction Threshold

One of these conditions is met:

- The rotation speed [Secondary Shaft Speed 1 (rpm)] measured by the CVT speed sensor is less than the rotation speed [Input Shaft (Drive Pulley) Speed rpm] measured by the CVT drive pulley speed sensor by a factor of 0.33 for at least 4 seconds.

- The rotation speed [Secondary Shaft Speed 1 (rpm)] measured by the CVT speed sensor is more than the rotation speed [Input Shaft (Drive Pulley) Speed rpm] measured by the CVT drive pulley speed sensor by a factor of 2.85 for at least 4 seconds.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- CVT speed sensor VEL-PWM line open (includes connector loose connection or disconnection)

- CVT speed sensor VEL-PWM line short to ground

- CVT speed sensor failure

- TCM internal circuit failure

Confirmation Procedure

Operating Condition

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Start the engine.

- Drive the vehicle at a speed of 37 mph (60 km/h) or more.

- Release the accelerator pedal, and slow the vehicle.

- Drive the vehicle in this manner only if the traffic regulations and ambient conditions allow.

With the HDS

None.

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Pending DTC, a Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data are stored in the TCM memory.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.
````

- The rotation speed [Secondary Shaft Speed 1 (rpm)] measured by the CVT speed sensor is more than the rotation speed [Input Shaft (Drive Pulley) Speed rpm] measured by the CVT drive pulley speed sensor by a factor of 2.85 for at least 4 seconds.

Possible Cause

Confirmation Procedure

Operating Condition

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Start the engine.

- Drive the vehicle at a speed of 37 mph (60 km/h) or more.

- Release the accelerator pedal, and slow the vehicle.

- Drive the vehicle in this manner only if the traffic regulations and ambient conditions allow.

With the HDS

None.

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Pending DTC, a Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data are stored in the TCM memory.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.

# DTC P0502 (K20C2)

DTC P0502: CVT Speed Sensor Circuit Open/Short

General Description

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The CVT speed sensor is attached to the outside of the transmission housing. The CVT speed sensor detects the number of revolutions of the secondary driven gear and sends a pulsing signal to the transmission control module (TCM). The TCM converts the pulsing signal into vehicle speed. If no pulses occur with the secondary driven gear rotating, the TCM detects a malfunction that may be caused by an open, an intermittent open, or a short to ground. If no signal from the CVT speed sensor is received for a set time, the TCM detects a malfunction and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Continuous

Sequence | None

Duration | 4 seconds or more

DTC Type | One drive cycle, MIL on, Shift position indicator blinks

Enable Conditions

Condition | Minimum | Maximum

Engine speed [Engine Speed] | 500 rpm | -

Drive pulley speed [Input Shaft (Drive Pulley) Speed rpm] | 200 rpm | -

12 volt battery voltage [Battery Voltage] | 10.0 V | -

Shift position/mode | Without paddle shifter | D, S, L

With paddle shifter | D, S

Other | No electrical noise in the CVT speed sensor output circuit

Decelerate and fuel cut-off for at least 3 seconds

[ ]: HDS Parameter

Malfunction Threshold

No signal from the CVT speed sensor is detected for at least 4 seconds.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- CVT speed sensor VEL-PWM line open (includes connector loose connection or disconnection)

- CVT speed sensor VCC line open (includes connector loose connection or disconnection)

- CVT speed sensor SG line open (includes connector loose connection or disconnection)

- CVT speed sensor VEL-PWM line short to ground

- CVT speed sensor failure

- TCM internal circuit failure

Confirmation Procedure

Operating Condition

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Start the engine.

- Drive the vehicle at a speed of 37 mph (60 km/h) or more.

- Release the accelerator pedal, and slow the vehicle.

- Drive the vehicle in this manner only if the traffic regulations and ambient conditions allow.

With the HDS

None.

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Pending DTC, a Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data are stored in the TCM memory.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.
````

## Chunk 9691: DTC P0600 (K20C2 (CVT System))

- Title: DTC P0600 (K20C2 (CVT System))

### Full Text

# DTC P0600 (K20C2 (CVT System))

DTC P0600: Serial Communication

General Description

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The driver IC which has diagnostic function and the CPU are built into the transmission control module (TCM). If the CPU cannot receive diagnostic information from the driver IC for a specified duration due to occurrence of communication abnormality between the driver IC and the CPU, the TCM detects a malfunction and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Continuous

Sequence | None

Duration | 5 seconds or more

DTC Type | One drive cycle, MIL on, Shift position indicator blinks

Enable Conditions

Condition

Other | When the vehicle is turned to the ON or OFF (LOCK) mode

Malfunction Threshold

One of the conditions is met for at least 5 seconds:

- The CPU does not receive signal from the driver IC via the communication lines.

- The signal sent from the driver IC is abnormal.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- TCM internal circuit failure

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Pending DTC, a Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data are stored in the TCM memory.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.
````

## Chunk 9693: DTC P0602 (K20C2 (CVT System))

- Title: DTC P0602 (K20C2 (CVT System))
- Source path: `pages\12299.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_dd0f02131b82`
- Images: `images\GHH399784.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\19428.html`

### Full Text

````text
# DTC P0602 (K20C2 (CVT System))

DTC P0602: Boot Mode (Programming Error)

General Description

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The transmission control module (TCM) is equipped with an update program to update its control program. The programs in the CPU of the TCM are classified as a TCM program (update-capable program) and a program for the update function (non-updatable program). The program update only updates the TCM program. When the TCM power is turned off during an update, the power for the update function is lost, and the update process stops. When the program update is stopped before it is completed, the TCM stores a DTC that indicates the update is not finished.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Continuous

Sequence | None

Duration | 1 second or less

DTC Type | One drive cycle, MIL on, Shift position indicator off

Enable Conditions

Condition

Vehicle | ON mode

Malfunction Threshold

The TCM program update stops 1 second before it is finished.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- TCM program unwritten

- TCM internal circuit failure

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When the TCM program is not written in the TCM, the MIL comes on and a Pending DTC and a Confirmed DTC are stored in the TCM memory. The shift position indicator does not blink.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The MIL, the Pending DTC, and the Confirmed DTC are cleared when the TCM program update is completed.
````

## Chunk 9695: DTC P062F (K20C2 (CVT System))

- Title: DTC P062F (K20C2 (CVT System))
- Source path: `pages\12301.html`

- Duplicate sources: `pages\19430.html`

### Full Text

````text
# DTC P062F (K20C2 (CVT System))

DTC P062F: TCM Internal Control Module Error

General Description

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The transmission control module (TCM) is equipped with a keep alive memory (KAM). The data (control learn data, etc.) for powertrain control and information (vehicle identification number (VIN), etc.) related to the vehicle control is stored in the KAM, so that it can be maintained even when power is not supplied to the TCM, such as when the 12 volt battery is disconnected. When powered up, the CPU retrieves the stored information from the KAM, as well as writes data to the KAM (control related data is written when the vehicle condition is turned to the ON mode, and vehicle information when commanded from the HDS). When the data retrieval or data writing process is not finished normally, the TCM detects a malfunction and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Continuous

Sequence | None

Duration | 1 second or more

DTC Type | One drive cycle, MIL on, Shift position indicator blinks

Enable Conditions

Condition

Other | When the vehicle is turned to the ON or OFF (LOCK) mode

Malfunction Threshold

A malfunction is detected whenever the keep alive data retrieval and writing process is not completed normally.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- TCM internal circuit failure

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Pending DTC, a Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data are stored in the TCM memory.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.
````

# DTC P0641 (K20C2 (CVT System))

DTC P0641: Sensor Reference Voltage 1 Circuit Malfunction

General Description

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The transmission control module (TCM) is equipped with a voltage monitor (5 V power source) to supply a stable 5 V to each sensor as reference voltage. The correct voltages for the sensors are loaded in the CPU of the TCM (A/D input) and when the sensor power voltage is a set value (high or low) for a certain time, the TCM detects a malfunction and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Continuous

Sequence | None

Duration | 2 seconds or more

DTC Type | One drive cycle, MIL on, Shift position indicator blinks

Enable Conditions

Condition

Vehicle | ON mode

Malfunction Threshold

The sensor power voltage is specified value or more, or specified value or less, for at least 2 seconds.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- CVT drive pulley speed sensor VCC line short to power

- CVT driven pulley pressure sensor VCC line short to power

- CVT drive pulley speed sensor VCC line short to ground

- CVT driven pulley pressure sensor VCC line short to ground

- TCM internal circuit failure

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Pending DTC, a Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data are stored in the TCM memory.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.
````

# DTC P06A8 (K20C2 (CVT System))

DTC P06A8: Internal VCC Power Malfunction

General Description

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The transmission control module (TCM) monitors the internal 5 V power supply by comparing it to the A/D input power supply voltage (3.3 V). When the 5 V power supply is normal, the comparison power supply voltage (3.3 V) falls within a normal area. But if the 5 V power supply malfunctions, the comparison power supply voltage (3.3 V) exceeds the range of normal area. If the comparison power supply voltage is out of permissible range for a set time, the TCM detects a malfunction and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Continuous

Sequence | None

Duration | 2 seconds or more

DTC Type | One drive cycle, MIL on, Shift position indicator blinks

Enable Conditions

Condition

Vehicle | ON mode

Malfunction Threshold

The TCM internal comparison power supply voltage is specified value or less, specified value or more for at least 2 seconds.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- TCM (internal 3.3 V or 5 V power supply circuit) failure

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Pending DTC, a Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data are stored in the TCM memory.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.

# DTC P0705 (K20C2)

DTC P0705: Transmission Range Switch Multiple Shift Position Input

General Description

Without paddle shifter

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

With paddle shifter

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Transmission range switch contact point input matrix

Without paddle shifter

Shift position/mode | Input per switch

ATP-P | ATP-R | ATP-RVS | ATP-N | ATP-D | ATP-S | ATP-L | ATP-FWD

P | O | X | X | X | X | X | X | X

R | X | O | O | X | X | X | X | X

N | X | X | X | O | X | X | X | X

Shift position/mode | Input per switch

ATP-P | ATP-R | ATP-RVS | ATP-N | ATP-D | ATP-S | ATP-L | ATP-FWD

D | X | X | X | X | O | X | X | O

S | X | X | X | X | X | O | X | O

L | X | X | X | X | X | X | O | O

With paddle shifter

Shift position/mode | Input per switch

ATP-P | ATP-R | ATP-RVS | ATP-N | ATP-D | ATP-S | ATP-FWD

P | O | X | X | X | X | X | X

R | X | O | O | X | X | X | X

N | X | X | X | O | X | X | X

D | X | X | X | X | O | X | O

S | X | X | X | X | X | O | O

O: Closed X: Open

The transmission range switch is attached to a control shaft. Operating the shift lever makes the control shaft rotate via the shift cable. The shift position indicator shows which position is selected according to the Low/High signal combinations, which vary based on the control shaft rotational angle. The control shaft changes the position of the transmission range switch, activates the manual valve, and switches hydraulic pressure to shift the transmission through forward/neutral/reverse. The transmission range switch signal is used to determine the shift schedule. The voltage is high (5 V or 12 V) at the transmission control module (TCM) input terminal when each transmission range switch position is open, and it is low (0 V) when each switch is closed. If the TCM detects multiple switch inputs instead of the correct switch input for the selected range at that time, it detects a malfunction and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Continuous

Sequence | None

Duration | 1 second or more

DTC Type | One drive cycle, MIL on, Shift position indicator blinks

Enable Conditions

Condition | Minimum | Maximum

12 volt battery voltage [Battery Voltage] | 10.0 V | -

[ ]: HDS Parameter

Malfunction Threshold

One of these conditions occurs.

- The TCM detects the selected range switch input and another range switch input (except FWD switch input [Forward Switch]) simultaneously for at least 1 second.

- The TCM detects the P, R, or N range switch input [A/T P, R, or N Switch] and the FWD switch input [Forward Switch] simultaneously for at least 1 second.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- Transmission range switch line short to ground

- Transmission range switch failure

- TCM internal circuit failure

Confirmation Procedure

Operating Condition

- Start the engine.

- With the brake pedal pressed, place the shift lever in each position for at least 1 second.

With the HDS

None.

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Pending DTC, a Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data are stored in the TCM memory.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.
````

# DTC P0706 (K20C2)

DTC P0706: Transmission Range Switch Open

General Description

Without paddle shifter

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

With paddle shifter

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Transmission range switch contact point input matrix

Without paddle shifter

Shift position/mode | Input per switch

ATP-P | ATP-R | ATP-RVS | ATP-N | ATP-D | ATP-S | ATP-L | ATP-FWD

P | O | X | X | X | X | X | X | X

R | X | O | O | X | X | X | X | X

N | X | X | X | O | X | X | X | X

Shift position/mode | Input per switch

ATP-P | ATP-R | ATP-RVS | ATP-N | ATP-D | ATP-S | ATP-L | ATP-FWD

D | X | X | X | X | O | X | X | O

S | X | X | X | X | X | O | X | O

L | X | X | X | X | X | X | O | O

With paddle shifter

Shift position/mode | Input per switch

ATP-P | ATP-R | ATP-RVS | ATP-N | ATP-D | ATP-S | ATP-FWD

P | O | X | X | X | X | X | X

R | X | O | O | X | X | X | X

N | X | X | X | O | X | X | X

D | X | X | X | X | O | X | O

S | X | X | X | X | X | O | O

O: Closed X: Open

The transmission range switch is attached to a control shaft. Operating the shift lever makes the control shaft rotate via the shift cable. The shift position indicator shows which position is selected according to the Low/High signal combinations, which vary based on the control shaft rotational angle. The control shaft changes the position of the transmission range switch, activates the manual valve, and switches hydraulic pressure to shift the transmission through forward/neutral/reverse. The transmission range switch signal is used to determine the shift schedule. The voltage is high (5 V or 12 V) at the transmission control module (TCM) input terminal when each transmission range switch position is open, and it is low (0 V) when each switch is closed. If the FWD switch stays open while the vehicle repeatedly accelerates to a specified vehicle speed and then stops despite being in D or S position/mode, the TCM detects a malfunction in the transmission range switch (open) and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Continuous

Sequence | None

Duration | Depending on the driving conditions

DTC Type | Two drive cycles, MIL on, Shift position indicator off

Enable Conditions

Condition

Shift position/mode | D, S

Malfunction Threshold

No FWD position signal [Forward Switch] is detected when the vehicle speed [Vehicle Speed] changes from 6 mph (10 km/h) -> 25 mph (40 km/h) -> 6 mph (10 km/h) in D or S position/mode.

[ ]: HDS Parameter

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- Transmission range switch ATP-FWD line open (includes connector loose connection or disconnection)

- Transmission range switch failure

- Shift cable adjustment failure

- TCM internal circuit failure

Confirmation Procedure

Operating Condition

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Start the engine.

- Accelerate the vehicle in D position/mode to 30 mph (48 km/h) or more.

- Slow down to 6 mph (10 km/h) for several seconds.

- Stop the vehicle, and turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

- Repeat Driving Pattern steps 1 through 3.

- Drive the vehicle in this manner only if the traffic regulations and ambient conditions allow.

With the HDS

None.

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected during the first drive cycle, a Pending DTC is stored in the TCM memory. If the malfunction returns in the next (second) drive cycle, the MIL comes on and a Confirmed DTC and the freeze data are stored. The shift position indicator does not blink.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.
````

## Chunk 9707: DTC P0710 (K20C2) (2018 2019 2020 2021)

- Title: DTC P0710 (K20C2) (2018 2019 2020 2021)

### Full Text

# DTC P0710 (K20C2) (2018 2019 2020 2021)

DTC P0710: CVT Fluid Temperature Sensor (Out of Range)

General Description

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The transmission fluid temperature sensor is a thermistor type sensor that changes resistance according to the change in transmission fluid temperature. The transmission control module (TCM) sends a 5 V reference signal to the sensor through a pull-up resistor. When the transmission fluid temperature is low, the sensor resistance increases and the TCM detects a high signal voltage. As the transmission fluid temperature rises, the sensor resistance decreases and the TCM detects a low signal voltage. When the transmission fluid temperature sensor signal voltage to the TCM is a specified range for a specified time, the TCM detects a malfunction and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Continuous

Sequence | None

Duration | 11 seconds or more

DTC Type | Two drive cycles, MIL on, Shift position indicator blinks

Enable Conditions

Condition | Minimum | Maximum

12 volt battery voltage [Battery Voltage] | 10 V | -

[ ]: HDS Parameter

Malfunction Threshold

One of these conditions is met for at least 11 seconds.

- The transmission fluid temperature sensor output voltage [CVTF Temp Sensor (V)] is in between 0.05 V and 0.09 V.

- The transmission fluid temperature sensor output voltage [CVTF Temp Sensor (V)] is in between 4.83 V and 4.92 V.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- Transmission fluid temperature sensor failure

Confirmation Procedure

Operating Condition

- Start the engine.

- Shift to P position/mode, and wait for at least 11 seconds.

With the HDS

None.

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected during the first drive cycle, a Pending DTC is stored in the TCM memory. If the malfunction returns in the next (second) drive cycle, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Confirmed DTC and the freeze data are stored.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.
````

# DTC P0711 (K20C2)

DTC P0711: CVT Fluid Temperature Sensor (Range/Performance)

General Description

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The transmission fluid temperature sensor is a thermistor type sensor that changes resistance according to the change in transmission fluid temperature. The transmission control module (TCM) sends a 5 V reference signal to the sensor through a pull-up resistor. When the transmission fluid temperature is low, the sensor resistance increases and the TCM detects a high signal voltage. As the transmission fluid temperature rises, the sensor resistance decreases and the TCM detects a low signal voltage. If the transmission fluid temperature sensor signal does not change, the TCM detects a malfunction and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Once per driving cycle

Sequence | None

Duration | 5 minutes or more

DTC Type | Two drive cycles, MIL on, Shift position indicator blinks

Enable Conditions

Condition | Minimum | Maximum

Engine coolant temperature [Engine Coolant Temperature] | 158 deg.F (70 deg.C) | -

Initial engine coolant temperature [Engine Coolant Temperature] | - | 95 deg.F (35 deg.C)

Transmission fluid temperature [CVTF Temperature] | - | 14 deg.F (-10 deg.C)

Initial transmission fluid temperature [CVTF Temperature] | - | 14 deg.F (-10 deg.C)

Vehicle speed [Vehicle Speed] | 19 mph (30 km/h) | -

Throttle position | 4 deg. | -

Other | After the engine coolant temperature [Engine Coolant Temperature] reaches the fixed value, both the vehicle speed [Vehicle Speed] and the throttle position meet the conditions for at least 5 minutes

[ ]: HDS Parameter

Malfunction Threshold

The current transmission fluid temperature [CVTF Temperature] is 14 deg.F (-10 deg.C) or less after Enable Conditions.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- Transmission fluid temperature sensor failure

- TCM internal circuit failure

Confirmation Procedure

Operating Condition

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Start the engine with the engine coolant temperature [Engine Coolant Temperature] at 95 deg.F (35 deg.C) or less.

- Warm up the engine until the engine coolant temperature [Engine Coolant Temperature] reaches 158 deg.F (70 deg.C) or more.

- Drive the vehicle at a speed of 19 mph (30 km/h) or more with the throttle position at 4 deg. or more for at least 5 minutes.

- Drive the vehicle in this manner only if the traffic regulations and ambient conditions allow.

With the HDS

None.

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected during the first drive cycle, a Pending DTC is stored in the TCM memory. If the malfunction returns in the next (second) drive cycle, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Confirmed DTC and the freeze data are stored.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.
````

# DTC P0711 (L15B7/L15BA/L15BY)

DTC P0711: CVT Fluid Temperature Sensor (Range/Performance)

General Description

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The transmission fluid temperature sensor is a thermistor type sensor that changes resistance according to the change in transmission fluid temperature. The transmission control module (TCM) sends a 5 V reference signal to the sensor through a pull-up resistor. When the transmission fluid temperature is low, the sensor resistance increases and the TCM detects a high signal voltage. As the transmission fluid temperature rises, the sensor resistance decreases and the TCM detects a low signal voltage. If the transmission fluid temperature sensor signal does not change, the TCM detects a malfunction and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Once per driving cycle

Sequence | None

Duration | 5 minutes or more

DTC Type | Two drive cycles, MIL on, Shift position indicator blinks

Enable Conditions

Condition | Minimum | Maximum

Engine coolant temperature [Engine Coolant Temperature] | 158 deg.F (70 deg.C) | -

Initial engine coolant temperature [Engine Coolant Temperature] | - | 95 deg.F (35 deg.C)

[ ]: HDS Parameter

Condition | Minimum | Maximum

Transmission fluid temperature [CVTF Temperature] | - | 14 deg.F (-10 deg.C)

Initial transmission fluid temperature [CVTF Temperature] | - | 14 deg.F (-10 deg.C)

Vehicle speed [Vehicle Speed] | 19 mph (30 km/h) | -

Throttle position | 4 deg. | -

Other | After the engine coolant temperature [Engine Coolant Temperature] reaches the fixed value, both the vehicle speed [Vehicle Speed] and the throttle position meet the conditions for at least 5 minutes

[ ]: HDS Parameter

Malfunction Threshold

The current transmission fluid temperature [CVTF Temperature] is 14 deg.F (-10 deg.C) or less after Enable Conditions.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- Transmission fluid temperature sensor failure

- TCM internal circuit failure

Confirmation Procedure

Operating Condition

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Start the engine with the engine coolant temperature [Engine Coolant Temperature] at 95 deg.F (35 deg.C) or less.

- Warm up the engine until the engine coolant temperature [Engine Coolant Temperature] reaches 158 deg.F (70 deg.C) or more.

- Drive the vehicle at a speed of 19 mph (30 km/h) or more with the throttle position at 4 deg. or more for at least 5 minutes.

- Drive the vehicle in this manner only if the traffic regulations and ambient conditions allow.

With the HDS

None.

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected during the first drive cycle, a Pending DTC is stored in the TCM memory. If the malfunction returns in the next (second) drive cycle, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Confirmed DTC and the freeze data are stored.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.
````

## Chunk 9711: DTC P0712, P0713 (K20C2)

- Title: DTC P0712, P0713 (K20C2)

# DTC P0712, P0713 (K20C2)

DTC P0712: CVT Fluid Temperature Sensor (Short)

DTC P0713: CVT Fluid Temperature Sensor (Open)

General Description

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The transmission fluid temperature sensor is a thermistor type sensor that changes resistance according to the change in transmission fluid temperature. The transmission control module (TCM) sends a 5 V reference signal to the sensor through a pull-up resistor. When the transmission fluid temperature is low, the sensor resistance increases and the TCM detects a high signal voltage. As the transmission fluid temperature rises, the sensor resistance decreases and the TCM detects a low signal voltage. When the transmission fluid temperature sensor signal voltage to the TCM is below specification or above specification, indicating that the temperature is above specification (a short to ground) or below specification (open), the TCM detects a malfunction and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Continuous

Sequence | None

Duration | 10 seconds or more

DTC Type | Two drive cycles, MIL on, Shift position indicator blinks

Enable Conditions

Condition | Minimum | Maximum

12 volt battery voltage [Battery Voltage] | 11 V | -

[ ]: HDS Parameter

Malfunction Threshold

DTC: P0712

The transmission fluid temperature sensor output voltage [CVTF Temp Sensor (V)] is less than 0.07 V for at least 10 seconds.

DTC: P0713

The transmission fluid temperature sensor output voltage [CVTF Temp Sensor (V)] is more than 4.93 V for at least 10 seconds.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

DTC: P0712

- Transmission fluid temperature sensor TATF line short to ground

DTC: P0713

- Transmission fluid temperature sensor TATF line open (includes connector loose connection or disconnection)

Common

- Transmission fluid temperature sensor failure

- TCM internal circuit failure

Confirmation Procedure

Operating Condition

- Start the engine.

- Shift to P position/mode, and wait for at least 10 seconds.

With the HDS

None.

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected during the first drive cycle, a Pending DTC is stored in the TCM memory. If the malfunction returns in the next (second) drive cycle, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Confirmed DTC and the freeze data are stored.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.
````

## Chunk 9713: DTC P0714 (K20C2)

- Title: DTC P0714 (K20C2)
- Source path: `pages\12319.html`
- Chunk ID: `chunk_871d5d963d81`
- Images: `images\GHH399816.jpeg`, `images\GHH399817.jpeg`
- Duplicate sources: `pages\19448.html`

### Full Text

````text
# DTC P0714 (K20C2)

DTC P0714: CVT Fluid Temperature Sensor (Intermittent Failure)

General Description

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

The transmission fluid temperature sensor is a thermistor type sensor that changes resistance according to the change in transmission fluid temperature. The transmission control module (TCM) sends a 5 V reference signal to the sensor through a pull-up resistor. When the transmission fluid temperature is low, the sensor resistance increases and the TCM detects a high signal voltage. As the transmission fluid temperature rises, the sensor resistance decreases and the TCM detects a low signal voltage. When the engine is stopped and enough time has passed, the transmission fluid temperature will equalize to the engine coolant temperature. When the temperature in the transmission fluid temperature sensor deviates from the temperature in engine coolant temperature (ECT) sensor 2 after the engine is stopped and enough time has passed, the TCM detects a malfunction and stores a DTC.

Monitor Execution, Sequence, Duration, DTC Type

Execution | Once per driving cycle

Sequence | P0111, P0112, P0113, P0116, P0117, P0118, P011A, P011B, P0125, P0128, P2183, P2184, P2185 are judged as OK

Duration | 10 seconds or more

DTC Type | Two drive cycles, MIL on, Shift position indicator blinks

Enable Conditions

Condition | Minimum | Maximum

Elapsed time after the vehicle condition is turned to the OFF (LOCK) mode | 6 hours | -

Other | Not to be towed during engine stop

Malfunction Threshold

The temperature in the transmission fluid temperature sensor deviates from the temperature in ECT sensor 2, less than -43 deg.F (-24 deg.C) or greater than 57 deg.F (32 deg.C)*.

*: Greater than 108 deg.F (60 deg.C) if engine block heater is equipped.

Possible Cause

NOTE: The causes shown may not be a complete list of all potential problems, and it is possible that there may be other causes.

- Transmission fluid temperature sensor failure

- TCM internal circuit failure

Confirmation Procedure

Operating Condition

- Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode in P position/mode, and wait for at least 6 hours.

- Start the engine in P position/mode, and wait for at least 10 seconds.

With the HDS

None.

Diagnosis Details

Conditions for setting the DTC

When a malfunction is detected during the first drive cycle, a Pending DTC is stored in the TCM memory. If the malfunction returns in the next (second) drive cycle, the MIL comes on, the shift position indicator blinks, and a Confirmed DTC and the freeze data are stored.

Conditions for clearing the DTC

The shift position indicator comes off by turning the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. The MIL is cleared if the malfunction does not return in three consecutive trips in which the diagnostic runs. The MIL, the Pending DTC, the Confirmed DTC, and the freeze data can be cleared with the scan tool Clear command.
````
